Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 5 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 5 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 5 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - If so, did those mood changes happen only when the feeling of being watched was present, or at other times too?\n\nPatient: I've never had high or irritable energy, but I've been feeling deeply depressed, sad, and exhausted for the past three months. The feeling of being monitored actually started before that, about five months ago. This means there were several weeks at the beginning where I felt watched but my mood was still normal.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling watched, have you had any other symptoms like hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or interfered with?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do sometimes hear voices whispering when no one is around, and I've occasionally seen shadows or figures that others can't see. However, I don't feel like anyone is controlling or interfering with my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Since your depression began, have there been at least 2 weeks when the voices or feeling of being watched continued even though your mood was back to normal or not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes, there have been times like that. There was a period of about three weeks where my mood actually felt relatively normal and I wasn't feeling depressed, but I still heard the whispering and felt like I was being watched.\n\nDoctor: Over the full time you’ve been ill, have the depression symptoms been present for most of the illness, or have the psychotic symptoms happened mostly on their own?\n\nPatient: The depression symptoms have definitely been present for most of the overall time I've been struggling. However, there were still those periods, like the few weeks at the very beginning and that other time I mentioned, where the voices and the feeling of being watched were happening completely on their own without the depression.\n\nDoctor: Since these symptoms started, have they caused a clear drop in how you function day to day, like trouble keeping up with work, school, relationships, or taking care of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest, and I haven't thought about whether my day-to-day functioning has dropped. I've just been trying to get through each day, so I haven't really paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me whether, since this started, you’ve had problems at work or school, pulled away from other people, or had more trouble taking care of yourself than before?"
  }
]

Yes, I have definitely pulled away from my friends and family and have been withdrawing from people over the past few months. I've also had a really hard time keeping up with my work and daily tasks because I just don't have the energy or motivation, and I feel constantly exhausted.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ psychotic_symptoms\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 5 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - It feels like there are groups of people tracking my every move, and I just can\n\nDoctor: Have you also had times when your mood was unusually high or irritable with lots of energy, or times when you felt very depressed? If so, did those mood changes happen only when the feeling of being watched was present, or at other times too?\n\nPatient: I've never had high or irritable energy, but I've been feeling deeply depressed, sad, and exhausted for the past three months. The feeling of being monitored actually started before that, about five months ago. This means there were several weeks at the beginning where I felt watched but my mood was still normal.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling watched, have you had any other symptoms like hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or feeling that your thoughts are being controlled or interfered with?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do sometimes hear voices whispering when no one is around, and I've occasionally seen shadows or figures that others can't see. However, I don't feel like anyone is controlling or interfering with my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Since your depression began, have there been at least 2 weeks when the voices or feeling of being watched continued even though your mood was back to normal or not depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes, there have been times like that. I've just been trying to get through each day, so I haven't really paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me whether, since this started, you’ve had problems at work or school, pulled away from other people, or had more trouble taking care of yourself than before?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have definitely pulled away from my friends and family and have been withdrawing from people over the past few months. I've also had a really hard time keeping up with my work and daily tasks because I just don't have the energy or motivation, and I feel constantly exhausted.\n\nDoctor: Even during times when you were not feeling depressed, did you still have low motivation, reduced emotional expression, or social withdrawal?\n\nPatient: Yes, even during those times when my mood was okay, I still had no motivation to do anything and felt very shut off from other people. ========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates":["Schizoaffective Disorder (Depressive Type)","Schizophrenia"],"note":"Previously the candidates were [\"Schizoaffective Disorder (Depressive Type)\", \"Schizophrenia\"], and they remain the same. The interview adds strong evidence for a primary psychotic-spectrum illness: persecutory delusions, auditory and visual hallucinations, social withdrawal, low motivation, and functional decline. Schizoaffective Disorder (Depressive Type) is supported because there is a major depressive episode present for most of the illness, along with at least 2 weeks of psychotic symptoms occurring without depression. Schizophrenia also remains possible because there are prominent psychotic symptoms, negative symptoms, and decline in functioning; however, the substantial depressive burden across most of the illness keeps schizoaffective high on the differential. No evidence supports bipolar-spectrum conditions, and the psychosis is too broad for Delusional Disorder.","is_final":false}

Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
